=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a tree with NK vertices. The vertices are numbered 1,2,\dots,NK, and the i-th edge (i=1,2,\dots,NK-1) connects vertices u_i and v_i bidirectionally.
Determine whether this tree can be decomposed into N paths, each of length K. More precisely, determine whether there exists an N \times K matrix P satisfying the following:

- P_{1,1}, \dots, P_{1,K}, P_{2,1}, \dots, P_{N,K} is a permutation of 1,2,\dots,NK.
- For each i=1,2,\dots,N and j=1,2,\dots,K-1, there is an edge connecting vertices P_{i,j} and P_{i,j+1}.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N K
u_1 v_1
u_2 v_2
\vdots
u_{NK-1} v_{NK-1}

Output

If it is possible to decompose the tree into N paths each of length K, print Yes. Otherwise, print No.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N
- 1 \leq K
- NK \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 1 \leq u_i < v_i \leq NK
- The given graph is a tree.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3 2
1 2
2 3
3 4
2 5
5 6

Sample Output 1

Yes

It can be decomposed into a path with vertices 1,2, a path with vertices 3,4, and a path with vertices 5,6.

Sample Input 2

3 2
1 2
2 3
3 4
2 5
3 6

Sample Output 2

No

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

For K>1, sum selected = K-1. Since each arm length >=1, selected count at most K-1 but path degree limits to 2. Actually if selected count=2, sum = K-1; if selected count=1, arm length=K-1. So closed condition: either one child arm length K-1, or two child arms lengths a,b with a+b=K-1. (For K=1, no arms). Good.

So DP reduces to sets F(v) and boolean C(v). Need compute efficiently for all nodes.

Naively sets of lengths could be large. Need exploit total vertices 2e5 and K up to 2e5. Maybe F(v) has small size? Let's analyze.

F(v) = {1} ∪ {1+a : ∃ child c with a∈F(c) and all other children closed}. But this definition assumes all other children closed. If there is any child c with F(c) containing a and all other children closed, then 1+a ∈ F(v). Also if all children closed, then 1 ∈ F(v) (v alone open segment). But wait if all children closed, F(v) includes 1. If not all children closed, can F(v) include 1? No, because if v open to parent with no child arm, then all children's subtrees must be closed (edge cut). So F(v) contains 1 iff C(c) true for all children c. Let's call all_closed.

But is it possible that a child subtree is neither closed nor has an open segment? If edge v-c is cut, child must be closed. If edge used, child must have open segment. Since every vertex covered, exactly one of these. So yes.

Thus F(v) = {1} if all children closed, plus for each child c and each a ∈ F(c), if all other children closed then 1+a ∈ F(v). But wait if selected child c is open, all other children must be closed. If multiple children have open possibilities, cannot select one and have others closed unless others closed. So F(v) can be nonempty only if the number of children with C(c)=false is at most 1? Let's see: For any valid configuration in subtree(v), every child subtree is either closed or open. For K>1, leaf C(c)=false (single vertex not K), F(c)={1}. So not closed. But for some node, C(c) true and F(c) nonempty? Let's see. If C(c) true, subtree(c) can be partitioned into complete K-paths with edge to parent cut. But could we instead leave an open segment from c to parent and close all other parts? That is F(c). If edge to parent used, open segment can be c alone? But then x-y must be closed? x-y length2 not K, impossible. c-x length2? y alone impossible. c-x-y length3 but open cannot be K? For open to parent, length must <=K-1=2, so no. So F(c)=empty. Is that always? If C(c)=true, the component containing c in closed partition uses all K vertices in a path from c downward (or c internal with two arms). If we instead open to parent, c would have parent edge plus maybe one child arm. Could we partition the rest into complete paths? Maybe possible in larger subtrees. Seems C true may imply F empty due to total subtree size? Let's examine.

If C(c)=true, then subtree(c) can be partitioned into complete K-paths. The component containing c has size K and is contained in subtree(c). The rest of subtree(c) is also partitioned into complete K-paths. Therefore total size of subtree(c) is a multiple of K. Let size S_c = mK. If F(c) nonempty with open segment length L (1<=L<=K-1), then subtree(c) is partitioned into one open segment of length L plus complete K-paths. Thus S_c ≡ L mod K. No by size mod. So they are mutually exclusive. Important.

This simplifies: For each child, either closed (C true, F empty) or open (C false, F nonempty). Actually a child subtree could be neither? Let's define state type:
- Type C: subtree size multiple of K and can be fully partitioned.
- Type O: subtree size not multiple? F nonempty; contains one open segment length L = S_v mod K? We don't need sets, only one possible length.

Need compute C(v) and r_v (open length) if possible.

Let's define for each node v:
- sz[v] = size of subtree.
- good[v] = C(v) true (subtree can be fully partitioned into K-paths, no open to parent). Then necessarily sz[v] % K == 0? If good true, yes all components size K, so sz multiple K.
- open[v] = r if F(v) = {r} nonempty, else 0/None. If open nonempty, sz[v] % K = r (1..K-1). Also open[v] is the length of the open segment from v to parent.

Can we compute bottom-up with just these? Need recurrence for good[v] and open[v].

At node v, for each child c, the child's state can be:
- good[c] true: edge v-c can be cut, child subtree closed.
- open[c] = r (1..K-1): edge v-c can be used, child contributes arm length r. But note if open[c] = r, then sz[c] % K = r. If child is open, it cannot be cut; it must be connected to v. If child is good, it can be cut; can it also be used as open? We argued F(c) empty if good true. So no.

Thus each child is either "closed" (good true) or "open" (open[c]=r). If neither, invalid.

At v, to form a valid state, we need choose some children to be open arms connected to v; all other children must be good. The number of open children selected depends on state:
- For open[v] (edge to parent used): v can have at most one open child. If no open child, open[v]=1 (v alone) and all children good. If one open child with arm r, open[v]=1+r, and all other children good. Also open[v] must be <= K-1 (since parent side). But if open[v] = K? Not allowed. Wait if one open child with r=K-1, and all other children good, then v plus child arm length K forms a complete closed path. So good[v] true. Good.
- For good[v] (edge to parent cut): v can have 0,1,2 open children. All other children good. Need total length of v's component = 1 + sum(open child arms) = K. Since open child arms r are positive. Thus:
   * 0 open children: possible only if K=1 (v alone). For K>1 no.
   * 1 open child: need r = K-1.
   * 2 open children: need r1 + r2 = K-1.
All other children good. Also open child arms must be from children with open state. If a child is good, cannot be open. So selection is among "open children" (children with open[c] nonempty). Children with good[c] true are forced closed. Children with neither invalid.

Thus each node's state depends only on the list of children that are open (not good), and their r values. Let's define:
- For node v, let B = list of children c with open[c] != None (open children). Let G = children with good[c] true. If any child neither, impossible.
- Also note if a child is open, its r = sz[c] % K. But we'll store.

Then:
open[v]:
- If len(B) == 0: open[v] = 1, provided all children good. (v alone open segment)

Wait earlier I said choose some children to be open; but child state open means it is already determined to connect to v. If we want a child to be cut, it must be good. Additionally if len(B)=0 then K=1. Is that correct? This is a major simplification. Then DP is deterministic bottom-up based on child states. But is it too restrictive? Let's test with sample 1.

This DP seems very simple: bottom-up, each node either good (closed) or open with length r = sz % K? Let's derive direct formulas.

- sz=1.

   - if sz_child % K == 0: child must be good.

This suggests we can compute bottom-up:
For each node v:

- Let r = S % K.

       child contributes arm r_c.

   open condition: len(B) <=1 and sumB <= K-2 (so 1+sumB <= K-1). If len(B)=0, sumB=0, open=1 (requires K>=2; if K=1 open length? K=1 special). If len(B)=1 with r, open=1+r if r<=K-2. If r=K-1, open invalid.

Now, is it sufficient to use only subtree size mod K to determine child state? We need prove by induction:
For any valid decomposition of subtree(v) with either closed or open to parent, the state is determined by size mod K. We already argued using component counts. Let's formalize:
- If subtree(v) is fully partitioned into K-paths, then sz[v] = K * (#components), so sz[v] ≡0 mod K.
- If subtree(v) has one open path segment of length L (1<=L<=K-1) plus some complete K-paths, then sz[v] ≡ L mod K, with L in 1..K-1. So L = sz[v] mod K. Thus open length unique.
Thus a node cannot be both good and open; and if sz mod K=0, only good possible; if nonzero, only open possible. This is independent of recurrence. So DP can be simplified further: We don't need store open length separately; it's just sz[v] % K. Need only compute good[v] boolean for nodes with sz mod K=0? But to compute good for a node with sz mod K=0, we need know whether children are good/open based on their sz mod K. For a child with sz mod K !=0, it is open with length = sz_child % K, but we need know if that open state is actually possible. Is open state always possible if the node's computed open condition holds? We need compute open_possible for all nodes, not just good. For nodes with sz mod K !=0, open_possible boolean can be computed. If false, invalid. For nodes with sz mod K=0, good boolean computed; open not possible. However, for a child with sz mod K !=0, we need open_possible[child] true. If open_possible false, invalid. For a child with sz mod K=0, need good[child] true.

Thus we can compute two booleans:
- good[v]: valid as closed (only relevant if sz[v] % K == 0; if sz%K !=0, good false automatically by size).
- open[v]: valid as open (only relevant if sz[v] % K !=0; if sz%K==0, open false automatically? Wait open length cannot be 0, so open false. But our recurrence open condition with lenB=0 and K? If sz%K=0, could open[v]=K? Not allowed. So open false.)
But for nodes with sz%K !=0, open[v] can be true/false. For nodes with sz%K=0, good[v] can be true/false.

Can we compute open[v] without knowing good of children? Need for each child:
- If sz_child % K == 0: require good[child] true.
- Else: require open[child] true.
Then B arms = residues of children with nonzero mod. Then:

good[v] = (len(B) <= 2) and (sumB == K-1) and all children valid. But note if len(B)=0, sumB=0; for K=1, K-1=0, good true. For K>1, false. If len(B)=1, sumB=K-1. If len(B)=2, sumB=K-1. Now, we need produce final Python code. But before final, let's thoroughly prove DP and handle edge cases.

Let's define for rooted tree:
For a node v, consider the subtree T_v. In a decomposition of the whole tree, the edge from v to its parent is either cut or used.
- If cut: all path components are entirely within T_v. Since every component has K vertices, |T_v| is multiple of K. We call v "closed" or good.
- If used: the path containing v crosses the boundary. Within T_v, the part of this path is a simple path with one endpoint at v (the endpoint adjacent to parent) and the other endpoint somewhere in T_v. Let its length (number of vertices) be l. All other vertices in T_v are partitioned into K-paths. Then |T_v| ≡ l mod K, and 1 ≤ l ≤ K-1. Thus l is uniquely |T_v| mod K. We call v "open" with length l.

For each child c:
- If |T_c| ≡ 0 mod K, then in any valid state of v, c cannot be open (because open length would be 0 mod K impossible). Therefore edge v-c must be cut, so c must be good.
- If |T_c| ≡ r ≠0, then c cannot be good, so edge v-c must be used, and c must be open with length r.

Thus the set of child arms attached to v is exactly the children with nonzero subtree size mod K, and their lengths are their residues. Let A be this multiset, sum s, count m.
For v to be open to parent:
- v can have at most one child arm (because v already uses parent edge; if one child arm, v has degree 2; if none, degree1). So m ≤ 1.
- The open segment length is 1 + s (if m=1) or 1 (if m=0). It must be at most K-1, so s ≤ K-2.
For v to be good:
- v has no parent edge, so v can have at most two child arms (path degree ≤2). Thus m ≤ 2.
- The component containing v has length 1 + s, must equal K, so s = K-1.
All children not in A must be good; children in A must be open. If any required child state is false, v invalid.

This is exactly algorithm.

Need consider if m=0 and s=0 for good with K=1. We special-case K=1. For K>1, good requires s=K-1≥1, so m≥1. Thus every closed component of size >1 has at least one arm. Good.

Star with 3 leaves is not a path (center degree3). DP root center: leaves open1, B=[1,1,1], sum3=K-1, m=3 >2, good false. No. Good.

But all vertices must be in paths of K; a dangling path of length <K cannot be a component. It must connect to parent to be completed. Thus open state necessary. Good.

Implementation details:
```
import sys
def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]; K = data[1]
    V = N * K
    if K == 1:
        print("Yes")
        return
    adj = [[] for _ in range(V+1)]
    idx = 2
    for _ in range(V-1):
        u = data[idx]; v = data[idx+1]; idx += 2
        adj[u].append(v); adj[v].append(u)
    parent = [0]*(V+1)
    parent[1] = -1
    order = [1]
    for v in order:
        for to in adj[v]:
            if to == parent[v]:
                continue
            parent[to] = v
            order.append(to)
    sz = [0]*(V+1)
    good = [False]*(V+1)
    open_ok = [False]*(V+1)
    for v in reversed(order):
        total = 1
        arm_cnt = 0
        arm_sum = 0
        valid = True
        for to in adj[v]:
            if parent[to] == v:
                total += sz[to]
                r = sz[to] % K
                if r == 0:
                    if not good[to]:
                        valid = False
                else:
                    if not open_ok[to]:
                        valid = False
                    arm_cnt += 1
                    arm_sum += r
        sz[v] = total
        if not valid:
            good[v] = False
            open_ok[v] = False
        else:
            open_ok[v] = (arm_cnt <= 1 and arm_sum <= K - 2)
            good[v] = (arm_cnt <= 2 and arm_sum == K - 1)
    print("Yes" if good[1] else "No")
if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
Need ensure parent array for root's children: In order loop, if to == parent[v] continue; but for root parent[1] = -1, all neighbors appended. For a node v, a neighbor that is not parent but already visited? In a tree, no. But consider if parent[to] already set due to another path? Tree no cycles, so fine. However, if input tree is not rooted and we use `for v in order:` while appending, Python for loop over list will include appended elements? Yes, in Python, for loop over list iterates by index and will see appended elements? Actually yes, it will continue until current index reaches len, so appended elements are visited. This is a common BFS/DFS stack using list as queue. It works. But to be safe, use `for v in order:` with order appended; yes it works. Alternatively:
```
stack = [1]
order=[]
while stack:
   v=stack.pop()
   order.append(v)
   for to in adj[v]:
      if to != parent[v]:
          parent[to]=v
          stack.append(to)
```
This is DFS and order is preorder; reversed(order) is postorder. This avoids for-over-appending maybe clearer. But with stack, need avoid revisiting parent only; in tree okay. Let's use stack to be explicit:
```
parent = [0]*(V+1)
parent[1] = -1
order = []
stack = [1]
while stack:
    v = stack.pop()
    order.append(v)
    for to in adj[v]:
        if to == parent[v]:
            continue
        parent[to] = v
        stack.append(to)
```
In a tree, no visited needed. But if root has neighbor, parent set. Since no cycles, no node will be encountered from two neighbors. Good. However, with stack, if we don't check visited, in a tree it's fine. But consider undirected tree, when at v, neighbors include children and parent. Children have parent not set yet. No cross edges. So okay. If there were cycles, would need visited, but tree guaranteed.

Potential issue: If using stack, order is not necessarily topological? It is DFS preorder; reversed order ensures children before parent because in a tree, parent is pushed before child, so parent appears before child in order; reversed gives child before parent. Good.

Now, let's think about if V=1, K=1? N=1,K=1,V=1, no edges. K==1 special prints Yes. If K>1, V>=2? Since N>=1,K>=2 => V>=2. Good.

Now, let's test with sample1 using code mentally:

Now, let's think about if root good true but some node open_ok false with sz mod nonzero? Then root would likely invalid because that node is in some child chain. Could parse streaming with sys.stdin.buffer.readline to avoid memory, but not needed. Let's use readline for simplicity and lower memory:
```
input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
N,K = map(int,input().split())
adj=[[] for _ in range(N*K+1)]
for _ in range(N*K-1):
    u,v=map(int,input().split())
    adj[u].append(v); adj[v].append(u)
```
This is fine and memory lower. Use this in final code. It avoids data list. Good.

Now, let's think about if the stack DFS with parent only might not visit all nodes if parent array initially 0 and a neighbor has label 0? no. But to be safe, we can use:
```
parent = [0]*(V+1)
parent[1] = -1
order = []
stack = [1]
while stack:
    v = stack.pop()
    order.append(v)
    for to in adj[v]:
        if to == parent[v]:
            continue
        parent[to] = v
        stack.append(to)
```
In a tree, this visits all. But if the tree is rooted at 1 and there is a node with label 0? no. Good.

Now, let's consider if a node has arm_count=2 and arm_sum=K-1, good true, but one of the open children has open_ok true and its open segment length r, but the child's open segment length r maybe not equal to sz_child%K due to our code not storing? We can prove by induction. In code, we don't check `open_ok[to]` against `sz[to]%K`; we just use r=sz[to]%K. If open_ok[to] true, it should be consistent. But let's consider if due to a bug, open_ok[to] could be true for a node with sz%K = r but open length not r? The recurrence for open_ok[to] sets true only if arm_count<=1 and arm_sum<=K-2. Then open length =1+arm_sum. Since all good children sizes multiples K, sz[to] =1+arm_sum+multiples K. Thus sz[to]%K =1+arm_sum (if <=K-1). So open length equals sz%K. Good. So no need.

Now, let's think about if a node has arm_count=2 and arm_sum=K-1, good true, but one of the open children has open_ok true and its open length r, but the child's open length r could be different from sz_child%K if the child's good children sizes not multiples K? But good children by definition good true implies sz%K=0. We only treat a child as good if sz_child%K==0 and good[child] true. If good[child] true, its size multiple K. Good.

Now, let's consider if a node has a child with sz%K==0 and good[child] false, but open_ok[child] true? We argued impossible. But if due to bug open_ok true with sz%K==0? Let's see recurrence: open_ok true requires arm_sum<=K-2, so sz%K=1+arm_sum nonzero. Thus open_ok false if sz%K==0. Good.

Now, let's think about if a node has a child with sz%K!=0 and open_ok[child] false, but good[child] true? good true implies sz%K=0, contradiction. So no.

Thus child state forced.

? Wait I need output final code. The above is my reasoning. Need final code only. But I haven't actually written final code in the response yet. I need ensure code is correct.

Let's produce final code block with solve function.

Before final, let's consider one more potential issue: The stack DFS without visited could fail if the tree is rooted at 1 and parent array initially 0; if a neighbor has label 0? no. But if the tree has V=1 and K>1 impossible? V=NK, if K>1 and N>=1, V>=2. So root has at least one edge? If N=1,K=2,V=2 yes. If N=1,K=1 special. Good.

Now, let's think about if K>1 and V=1 impossible because N*K=1 implies K=1,N=1. So no.

Now, let's think about if input tree has NK vertices and NK-1 edges, but if K==1 and we return early, we don't read edges. That's okay. But if the judge expects program to read all input before output? No, output can happen before reading all input; process exits. Fine.

Now, let's think about if using `input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline` and K==1, we read first line, print Yes, return. The remaining input is unread but program ends. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possibility of recursion limit? no.

Now, let's write final code:

```
import sys

def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, K = map(int, input().split())
    if K == 1:
        print("Yes")
        return
    V = N * K
    adj = [[] for _ in range(V + 1)]
    for _ in range(V - 1):
        u, v = map(int, input().split())
        adj[u].append(v)
        adj[v].append(u)

    parent = [0] * (V + 1)
    parent[1] = -1
    order = []
    stack = [1]
    while stack:
        v = stack.pop()
        order.append(v)
        for to in adj[v]:
            if to == parent[v]:
                continue
            parent[to] = v
            stack.append(to)

    sz = [0] * (V + 1)
    good = [False] * (V + 1)
    open_ok = [False] * (V + 1)

    for v in reversed(order):
        total = 1
        arm_cnt = 0
        arm_sum = 0
        valid = True
        for to in adj[v]:
            if parent[to] == v:
                total += sz[to]
                r = sz[to] % K
                if r == 0:
                    if not good[to]:
                        valid = False
                else:
                    if not open_ok[to]:
                        valid = False
                    arm_cnt += 1
                    arm_sum += r
        sz[v] = total
        if not valid:
            good[v] = False
            open_ok[v] = False
        else:
            open_ok[v] = (arm_cnt <= 1 and arm_sum <= K - 2)
            good[v] = (arm_cnt <= 2 and arm_sum == K - 1)

    print("Yes" if good[1] else "No")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```

I'm going in circles. The code is correct.

Let's now prepare final answer with code block.</think>```python
import sys

def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.buffer.readline
    N, K = map(int, input().split())

    if K == 1:
        print("Yes")
        return

    V = N * K
    adj = [[] for _ in range(V + 1)]

    for _ in range(V - 1):
        u, v = map(int, input().split())
        adj[u].append(v)
        adj[v].append(u)

    parent = [0] * (V + 1)
    parent[1] = -1
    order = []
    stack = [1]

    while stack:
        v = stack.pop()
        order.append(v)
        for to in adj[v]:
            if to == parent[v]:
                continue
            parent[to] = v
            stack.append(to)

    sz = [0] * (V + 1)
    good = [False] * (V + 1)      # subtree can be fully decomposed
    open_ok = [False] * (V + 1)   # subtree can have one open path to parent

    for v in reversed(order):
        total = 1
        arm_cnt = 0
        arm_sum = 0
        valid = True

        for to in adj[v]:
            if parent[to] == v:
                total += sz[to]
                r = sz[to] % K

                if r == 0:
                    if not good[to]:
                        valid = False
                else:
                    if not open_ok[to]:
                        valid = False
                    arm_cnt += 1
                    arm_sum += r

        sz[v] = total

        if not valid:
            good[v] = False
            open_ok[v] = False
        else:
            open_ok[v] = (arm_cnt <= 1 and arm_sum <= K - 2)
            good[v] = (arm_cnt <= 2 and arm_sum == K - 1)

    print("Yes" if good[1] else "No")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
